2026. 10.27
先週（10/19～10/26）発表の主な経済指標
米生産活動は堅調。AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子
製品や機械などがけん引。一方、データセンター建設に伴う需要
増加が、他産業の生産や建設活動を圧迫する兆候も
コア消費者物価は再び2％超え。特殊要因を除くサービス価格
も加速し、人件費転嫁の兆し。連合は2027年春闘で6％中心
を要求。中小企業の余力に課題も、物価上振れに注意
注目点② サービス物価の加速で重み増す2027年春闘戦線注目点①AI投資の恩恵拡大、副作用もじわり顕在化
総合PMIは57.6と高水準を維持。新規失業保険申
請件数も19.4万件と低水準で解雇の動きは引き続
き限定的。一方で、住宅着工件数や住宅建設業
者の景況感は悪化、高金利の影響が継続

耐久財製造業は、軍需やデータセンター（DC）増
設の旺盛な需要が、他の製品の生産を圧迫して

（2026/10） テクノロジー企業がDC向け電気設備の買い占め

し、これを拒否

ベージュブック
（2026/7）
米国：AI関連を中心に製造業生産は堅調も、資材・設備の需給ひっ迫が他産業に波及
 米国の製造業生産は、AI関連投資を背景にコンピューター・電子製品、一次金属、一般機械などが堅調
― 一方、非耐久財や家具など消費関連の生産は軟調に推移し、業種間のばらつきが拡大
 FRBは、データセンター建設に伴う鋼材・電気設備などの需要増加が、他の建設案件や生産活動に影響していると指摘
―AI投資の拡大が関連産業の生産を押し上げる一方、供給制約を通じて他産業の活動を圧迫する兆候も
― 当面はAI投資による成長押し上げ効果が期待される一方、供給制約や価格上昇を通じた負の影響にも注意が必要
4

日本：全国コアCPIは再び2％超え。基調的なサービス価格も伸び高まる
5
 9月の全国コアCPIは前年比＋2.1％（前月：同＋1.7％）と加速。2025年12月以来の2％超に
― 原油高・円安等のコスト上昇もあり、家事用消耗品や教養娯楽用品等を中心に伸び率が拡大
― 一般サービス価格も伸び加速。シルバーウィークの並びによる宿泊料や外食費の上昇などが主因
 特殊要因を除いた一般サービス価格もじわり上昇
― 変動の大きい宿泊費や私立高校無償化による押し下げを除いた基調的な一般サービス価格に上昇圧力。人件費増の
価格転嫁が進んでいる可能性を示唆

全国コアCPI CPI一般サービス価格の推移

### Page 7

日本：連合は2027年春闘で賃上げ要求を強化。高水準の賃上げ持続と物価への波及が焦点
6
 連合は、2027年春闘賃上げ要求の目安を「6％中心、5％以上」と発表
―5％以上の賃上げを最低目標として明確化。実質賃金1％上昇の定着をめざす方針
― 中小企業は大企業との格差是正に向け「6％以上」が目標。過去4年のインフレで実質賃金が低下した分の回復も要求
― また、新たに、2030年に2026年の賃金水準を15％程度上回る中期目標を設定。持続的な賃金上昇をめざす
 賃上げ余力は企業規模・業種で濃淡。中小企業等で高い「賃上げ5％の壁」
― なお、6％に近い賃上げが実現する場合、サービス価格への波及が強まり、基調的物価上昇率が2％を上回る可能性
連合・春闘基本構想の新旧比較 春闘賃上げ率

基本構想」 より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

欧州：2027年度予算審議が本格化。生活苦によるデモが拡大する中、財政赤字は高水準
7
 2027年度予算審議が本格化。財政赤字は総じて高止まり
― 一定の防衛費・エネルギー関連支出を柔軟化する特例措置の適用後も、複数の国でEU財政基準で定める赤字幅を超
過。スペインは予算案を巡って議会解散。フランス・ベルギーも10/15時点でEUに予算案を未提出
 生活改善を求める抗議活動が欧州各国で発生。支出圧力の高まりは財政再建の障壁に
― フランスの学生によるデモを発端に、生活費の高騰や公共サービスの劣化に対する抗議活動は欧州各国に拡大。一部
は暴動に発展し、警察官と衝突する事態に
9～10月に発生した主な抗議活動主要国の財政収支実績と見通し
（出所） 各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

欧州：金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を嫌気。不透明感は2027年入り後も継続する見込み
8
 金融市場は政治・財政を巡る混乱を警戒。主要国の選挙を控える中、不透明感は2027年まで継続へ
― 抗議活動が広がるイタリア、フランス、ベルギーの一般政府債務残高（GDP比）は、EU加盟国の中でも上位（それぞれ2
位、3位、4位）。金利上昇の背景は政治・財政への懸念
― 影響は為替市場にも波及。9月中旬以降、ユーロはドルに対して弱含み

26年1月 株価指数算出会社が株式保有構造の不透明さを問題視し、市場区分引き下げの可能性を警告

26年6月

の確認が必要と指摘。改善が不十分なら格下げを検討する
と警告

## Document 062: 2026年7-9月期の実質GDP～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7-9月期の実質GDP～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

（前期比、％）
（年・四半期）
実質ＧＤＰ成長率の推移
民間最終消費支出 民間企業設備 その他民需 公需 外需
（資料）内閣府経済社会総合研究所「四半期別ＧＤＰ速報」
( )内は年率換算値
(注）2602までは当研究所による改定見込値、2603は予測値
予測

１. 11/16に内閣府から公表される 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、前期比▲0.1％（前期比年
率▲0.3％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長になったと推計される。 
２. 高水準の企業収益を背景に設備投資が前期比 1.4％と大幅に増加したが、天候不順の影響
などから民間消費が前期比▲0.3％と減少したこと、原油を中心に輸入が大幅に増加し、
外需が成長率を大きく押し下げたことなどから、小幅ながら 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成
長になったとみられる。 
３. 名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質GDPの伸
びを大きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％、前年比 2.5％と予測する。 
４. 2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時
的な影響が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回
復を妨げる可能性が高い。 
５. 現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むこ
となどから、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまる
と予想している。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-10-30

2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP 
～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎 
(03)3512-1836  tsaito@nli-research.co.jp

### Page 2

名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率 2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質 GDPの伸びを大
きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％（4-6月期：同 1.0％）、前年比 2.5％（4-6月
期：同 2.6％）と予測する。

なお、11/16に内閣府から 2026年 7-9月期の GDP速報が発表される際には、基礎統計の改定や季
節調整のかけ直しなどから、成長率が過去に遡って改定される。当研究所では、2026年 4-6月期の
実質 GDP成長率は、外需、設備投資が上方修正されることから、前期比年率 1.4％から同 2.3％へ
上方修正されると予想している。

2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時的な影響
が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回復を妨げる可能性が
高い。消費者物価上昇率は 2026年 9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となったが、原材料価格の上昇を耐久
財や日用品などの販売価格に転嫁する動きが今後さらに広がることから、2026年度末にかけて 3％
台まで高まる公算が大きい。物価上昇率の高まりによる実質購買力の低下を背景に 2026年度後半
の民間消費は弱めの動きが続くことが予想される。 
現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むことなどか
ら、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまると予想している。

1 10/30までに公表された経済指標をもとに予測している。今後公表される経済指標の結果によって予測値を修正する可
能性がある。

### Page 3

民間消費は前期比▲0.3％と予測する。 
実質賃金はプラスの伸びが続いているが、物価上昇率の高まりによって実質購買力が押し下げら
れていることに加え、台風や豪雨、長雨などの天候不順が個人消費を下押しした。 
2026年 7-9月期の消費関連指標を確認すると、延べ宿泊者数（うち日本人）は前期比 2.8％（4-6
月期：同▲0.9％）の増加となったが、自動車税の
「環境性能割」の廃止を受けて 4-6月期に前期比
10.7％の高い伸びとなった自動車販売台数が、その
反動で同▲7.5％と落ち込んだほか、外食産業売上
高が前期比▲0.3％（4-6月期：同 0.2％）、小売業
販売額指数が前期比▲2.1％（4-6月期：同 0.6％）
と減少した（いずれもニッセイ基礎研究所による
季節調整値、外食産業売上高、小売業販売額指数
は消費者物価指数で実質化）。

・住宅投資～弱めの動きが続く～

住宅投資は前期比▲0.2％と 2四半期連続の減
少を予測する。 
新設住宅着工戸数（季節調整済・年率換算値）
は、建築物省エネ法・建築基準法改正前の駆け
込み需要の反動で、2025年 1-3月期の 86.7万戸
から 4-6月期に 62.0万戸へ急減した後、7-9月
が 71.9万戸と持ち直し、その後は概ね 70万戸
台半ばで推移している。 
GDP統計の住宅投資は住宅価格上昇の影響も
あり弱めの動きとなっている。

・民間設備投資～高水準の企業収益を背景に増加～

民間設備投資は前期比 1.4％と 3四半期ぶりの増加を予測する。 
設備投資の一致指標である資本財総供給は 2026年 4-6月期の前期比 4.9％の後、7-9月期も同 4.9％
となった。また、機械投資の先行指標である機械受注（船舶・電力を除く民需）は 2026年 4-6月
期に前期比 0.2％と 3四半期連続で増加した後、7、8月の平均は 4-6月期を 0.4％上回っている。 
日銀短観 2026年 9月調査では、2026年度の設備投資計画（含むソフトウェア・研究開発投資額、
除く土地投資額）が6月調査から 0.5％上方修正され、前年度比9.3％（全規模・全産業）となった。 
設備投資は、高水準の企業収益を背景に、人手不足対応の省力化投資、デジタル化に向けた情報

・公的固定資本形成～2四半期連続の減少～

公的固定資本形成は、前期比▲2.4％と予測する。 
公共工事の先行指標である公共工事請負金額
は 2026年 7-9月期に前年比▲2.6％と 6四半期ぶ
りに減少した。また、公共工事の進捗を反映する
公共工事出来高（建設総合統計）は、2026年 4-6
月期に前年比 5.2％と 9四半期連続で増加した後、
2026年 7、8月の平均は同 2.4％と伸びが鈍化し
ている。 
2025年度補正予算による押し上げ効果が弱ま
っていることに加え、2026 年度当初予算の公共
事業関係費の伸びが抑えられていることから、弱
めの動きとなっている。

・外需～4四半期ぶりのマイナス寄与～

外需寄与度は前期比▲0.4％（前期比年率▲1.8％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナスを予測する。財貨・
サービスの輸出が前期比 2.0％、財貨・サービスの輸入が同 4.8％となるだろう。代替調達の進展に
伴う原油輸入量の急増を主因として 輸入が高い伸
びとなり、輸出の伸びを上回ったことから、外需は
成長率の押し下げ要因となった。 
2026年 7-9月期の地域別輸出数量指数を季節調整
値（当研究所による試算値）でみると、米国向けが
前期比 0.9％（4-6月期：同 2.8％） 、EU向けが前期
比▲2.4％（4-6月期：同 0.3％） 、アジア向けが前期
比▲0.3％（4-6 月期：同▲0.8％） 、うち中国向けが

## Document 148: 261030_ubs_MTK

### Page 1

ab 30 October 2026
Global Research
Key Call: MediaTek Inc.
Raising TPU guidance with stronger execution
TPU v9 on track to ramp from early 2028
Amid market skepticism on TPU execution, MediaTek delivered better outlook for Q4
and Cloud ASICs, as we previewed. Management raised TPU sales guidance from >US
$2bn to US$2.5bn for 2026, and from US$12-16bn to US$16bn for 2027. This reflects
the steady Google demand for v8t for training, critical to accelerate the new Gemini LLM

development. Importantly, for TPU v9, MediaTek re-affirmed it is on track for high
volume manufacturing in Q128, which clarified  recent noises around delayed tape out.
Our latest understanding is there may be two versions of MediaTek’s TPU v9: the first
version with 336G SerDes should be tape out soon in Nov 2026. There’s a second tape
out which is on track for delivery in Feb 2027. The second version features flexible
SerDes switch between 224G/336G for greater supply flexibility for the whole system.

Conclusion is, we think MediaTek is on good track to scale TPU  sales to our estimates of
US$44bn/53bn in 2028/29  with upside. See Figure 1U B S  f o r e c a s t s  f o r   M e d i a T e k ' s  T P U  b u s i n e s s for our TPU financial analysis.
Thoughts on the second Cloud ASIC client
MediaTek announced the 2nd Cloud ASIC client, which should contribute to revenue in
2028. MediaTek clarified it’s not a Chinese customer. We believe there are 2 higher

possibilities as what we flagged recently. 1) Tesla’s AI6 using Intel 14A, produced at
Terafab ramping in H228. Potential revenue opportunity on full year basis could be ~US
$2bn if MediaTek could ramp 1m units. Margin profile could be lower than TPU's 42%
GM given simpler design. 2) SpaceX/xAI's Dojo3. MediaTek should be partnering with
Nvidia to offer NVLink Fusion for faster time to market. Question is SpaceX/xAI's own
engineering resource and capability for Dojo3 after team reshuffle. If secured, our initial

estimate is revenue opportunity a year could be US$4-9bn, assuming good volume.
Margin profile may be similar or higher than TPU, in our view.
Meaningful operating margin expansion ahead
Q4 sales are guided to grow 28% QoQ at the mid point, beating consensus of 25%,
even with higher base from Q3. GM is guided to edge down to 45.0% at mid point, with
OPM meaningfully rising to 19.0% from 16.1% in Q3. As MediaTek starts ramping TPU
substantially into 2027, the key focus for profitability should shift from GM to OPM due

to different business model vs consumer electronics, and our sense is the market still
under appreciates the structural shift and the stronger earnings power. We forecast
MediaTek's OPM to rise to 25.2%/30.9% in 2027/28 vs consensus of 21.8%/24.2%.
Our 2027/28E EPS estimates are 23%/29% above consensus pre earnings.
Valuation: maintain Key Call Buy and target of NT$7,300
Among our coverage, MediaTek remains the one that we believe the market most

underestimates its execution and opportunity in Cloud AI. We raise 2026E EPS by 5%
and fine tune 2027/28E EPS by 1-2%. Maintain Key Call Buy. Our target of NT$7,300 is
based on 27x 2027-28E PE.

Company Description
MediaTek is a leading integrated circuit (IC) design company in Taiwan, focusing on solutions
from the edge to the cloud, applications for its product offerings range from smartphones,
smart homes and AI PCs to high-performance computing, automotive, and AI data centers.

Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target for MediaTek is based on a target PE methodology.
Fabless integrated circuit (IC) design houses face a variety of uncertainties, including rapidly
changing technology, end-market demand, competition, employee turnover and foundry
wafer costs. They are also vulnerable to legal disputes over intellectual property rights. In
addition, MediaTek is exposed to potential price competition in handset SoC, TV SoC, etc.

Downside risks include slower-than-expected market share gains and a weaker-than-
expected demand recovery.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

주가 추이

3Q26 매출액과 영 업 이 익 은 모두 당사 추정치를 크게 하회했습니다. 탁월
한 차량 판매 증가세에도 불구하고 부정적 믹스와 환율/원자재가 상승에 따
른 수익성 악화가 아 쉬 웠 습 니 다. 매크로 영향 고려한 추 정 치 하향 및 로봇 
지분가치 할인으로 목표주가 21만원으로 조정합니다. 
큰 폭의 판매 성장에도 수익성 크게 악화 
동사 3Q26 매출액은 31.6조원(+10.1% 이하 YoY), 영업이익 1.8조원
(+24.3%)을 기록하며 추정치를 크게 하회했다. 관세 영향이 본격화되었
던 전년 동기 대비 관세율 차이에 따른 비용 감소(5.3천억원)와 판매보증 
충당부채 전입액 감소(6.7천억원) 등 낮은 기저로 이익이 개선되었으나, 
2023년 생산 정상화 이후 가장 큰 폭의 판매 성장(84.7만대, +7.9%)에도 
불구하고 인센티브와 재료비 상승, 중동 물량 감소와 경제형 EV 판매 확
대에 따른 부정적 믹스로 OPM 5.8%에 그친 실적을 기록했다.  
예상보다 큰 부정적 PT/지역 믹스 영향 
기대를 상회하는 판매량 증가에 따라 물량 효과 통한 수익성 개선을 기
대했으나, 예상 대비 큰 3Q 부정적 믹스 영향에 따라 4Q와 ’27년 실적 
개선 기대 또한 낮출 수밖에 없는 상황이다. 아중동 판매 부진과 유럽 모
델 수익성 저하 영향이 판매 물량 증가 효과보다 크게 작용하고 있다. 인
도, 중남미 대비 고마진 차종 믹스가 좋은 중동의 지정학적 영향이 지속
되고 있고, 유럽은 EV2 등 신차 중심 판매 확대에도 중국 로컬업체들의 
유럽 공세 격화로 시장 점유율을 사수하기 위한 모델 저수익화가 예상 
대비 장기화될 것으로 판단한다. 그러나 고정비 만회를 위해 인센티브 
증가와 가격 효과 축소 영향 속에서도 판매 확대는 불가피한 상황이다. 
부정적 상황 장기화 가능성, 목표주가 210,000원으로 하향 
결국, 수익성 개선을 위해서는 고마진 제품 판매 확대 또는 판매가 견조
하나 저마진 차종 원가를 절감하는 방법밖에 없다. 북미 텔루라이드 
HEV 판매와 스포티지HEV HMGMA 증산이 고마진 물량 증가에 지속
기여할 것이다. 그러나 단기 중동 시장 회복과 차종별 원가절감 효과 확
인이 어렵다는 점에서 고마진 제품 판매 확대 효과는 제한적 전망이다. 
이에, 투자의견 Buy 유지하되, 목표주가 21만원으로 조정한다. 실적 추
정치 조정하여 영업가치를 종전 대비 15.7% 하향하고, 성장주 밸류 디스
카운트에 따른 로봇 지분가치 30% 할인하였다.

이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 7

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

7 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

조선/방산. 한승한, RA. 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 1,300,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 760,000 원 
상승여력: 71.1%

Signal: 3Q26, 수출 물량 저조 및 국내 낮은 마진 물량 인식으로 어닝 쇼크 
Key: 4Q부터 내년까지 수출 물량 빠르게 회복하며 실적 성장 이어갈 전망 
Step: 연내 새로운 중장기 성장동력이 될 중동 수주계약 기대. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 수출 물량 납품 저조로 어닝 쇼크  
3Q26 연결 매출액 6 조 943 억원(-6.0% YoY, -34.4% QoQ), 영업이익 4,520억원
(-46.2% YoY, -66.9% QoQ)으로, 시장예상치(1조 1,615억원)를 대폭 하회하는 어닝 쇼
크 기록했다. ①폴란드 중심의 수출 물량의 매출 인식 시점이 4 분기로 이연되며, 수출 물량 
비중이 감소한 점이 컸다. ②내수 물량 또한 감소했으며, 특히 국내 물량 중에서도 상대적
으로 마진율 낮은 유무인 무기체계 초도 물량이 이번 분기에 집중됐다. ③내수&수출 전반
적인 지상방산 매출 비중 감소에 따른 고정비 부담이 컸으며, ④일회성 요인으로 대전 사업
장 사고에 따른 복구 비용 약 300 억원과 충당금 및 기타 비용 총 160 억원, 그리고 환율 
하락으로 영업이익의 약 10% 수준의 감소 효과 발생했다. 다만 4Q26 부터 이번 분기 이
연된 폴란드 중심의 수출 물량 인식이 반영되면서 큰 폭의 탑라인 및 이익 성장 시현할 전
망이며, 대전 사업장 또한 내년 상반기 가동 재개 예상됨에 따라 지상방산 부문은 전년 대비 
성장 폭 두드러지며 사측 가이던스 15% 매출 성장 충분히 달성 가능할 전망이다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 130 만원으로 하향 
한화에어로스페이스에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가 기존 160 만원에서 130
만원으로 하향한다. 최근 Peer 그룹의 주가 하락에 따라 멀티플 하향 조정했기 때문이다. 
폴란드 천무 유도탄 JV에 이어 현지화가 전제된 K9 3차 계약을 추진 중이며, 국내 장약 생
산능력 증설을 통한 유럽향 수요 대응과 미국 내 생산거점 구축까지 진행 중에 있다. 또한 
미국 차륜형 자주포 사업 시제 선정에 이은 궤도형 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다. 중동 국
가들의 방공무기체계에 대한 계약 논의는 매우 가시적인 상황이며, 사우디 MNG 사업 수주 
또한 시점의 문제일 뿐이다. 기존 유럽에 더해 중동은 현지화 전략을 기반으로 동사의 새로
운 중장기 성장동력이 될 지역이며, 올해 4 분기부터 해당 수주 모멘텀과 함께 큰 폭의 실적 
성장이 맞물리며 리레이팅 가능할 전망이다. 선제적 매수 기회라는 판단이다.

## Document 238: [034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한_07bab1f0

Source PDF: all report pdf/[034020] 두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락두산에너빌리티(034020) 펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한_07bab1f0.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(034020) 
두산에너빌리티 
2026.10.30

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

펀더멘털은 견고, 노이즈로 인한 일시적 주가 하락 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 80,000원(하향) 
 현재주가 56,700 원(10/29)

 [투자의견] 당사는 두산에너빌리티에 대해 목표주가를 80,000원(2027년 
BPS의 6.2배)으로 27% 하향하며, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 실적은 견조
한 회복을 보이고 있으나, 최근 GE Vernova(GEV) 등 글로벌 AI 및 에너지 
밸류체인 Peer 주가 조정을 반영해 멀티플을 하향 조정했다. 체코 시공 계
약 등 일부 노이즈에 의해 주가가 급락한 만큼 매수 기회라고 판단한다.  
 [3Q26 Review]  매출액 4.4조원(-5.8%qoq, +14.7%yoy), 영업이익 
2,612억원(-16.9%qoq, +90.6%yoy)을 기록해 당사 추정에 부합하는 무
난한 실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티 부문은 매출액 1.8조원, 영업이익 1,211
억원(영업이익률 6.6%, +2.3%p qoq)을 기록하며 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 
고수익 사업 믹스 개선으로 분기 수익성이 재차 개선됐다. 당기순이익은 
149억원(-93.4%qoq)에 그쳤으나, 원달러 환율 급락으로 발생한 환손실 
반영 때문으로 이익 체력과는 무관하다고 판단한다. 3Q26 미국 빅테크 데
이터센터향 가스터빈 첫 출하를 달성했으며, 수주잔고는 에너빌리티 부문 
기준 26.6조원(+70.9%yoy)으로 사상 최대치를 경신했다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 4.8조원, 영업이익 2,640억원으로 유사한 실적 
흐름을 전망한다. 4Q 가스터빈 인도와 체코 두코바니 원전 진행률 반영으로 
에너빌리티 부문 외형 성장이 기대된다. 3Q26 별도 기준 영업이익률이 
8.5%까지 개선된 만큼 에너빌리티 부문의 분기 실적 개선 추세는 유효하다
고 판단한다. 연간 수주 목표 13.3조원 달성을 위한 잔여 수주(3.3조원 +α)
가 예정되어 있으며, 대형원전을 제외하더라도 가이던스 달성은 무난할 것
이라 판단한다. 세부적으로는 SMR 1조원(계약 조건 합의 완료 단계로 연내 
체결 가능), 신재생 7,000억원(해상풍력 기자재·EPC 2건 협의 완료 단계), 
해외 복합 EPC 5,000억원(국내 1건, 해외 2건)이 기대된다. 
 [가스터빈] 3Q26 누적 가스터빈 13기·스팀터빈 11기·LTSA 6건을 달성하
며 연간 10기 이상 수주·수행 역량이 안정적으로 유지되고 있다. 2030년 
슬롯 예약 물량의 본계약 전환은 2027년, 2031년 슬롯은 2028년으로 예
상되며, 수주잔고 증가가 기대된다. 가스터빈 수요 증가를 감안하면 추가 캐
파 증설 결정이 이르면 연내 이루어질 전망이다. 
 [원전] 10월 NuScale 1개 모듈 제작 계약을 체결했으며, PPA 지연 여부와 
무관하게 완결된 계약으로 실적에 반영될 전망이다. X-energy는 연내 기자
재 및 부품의 후속 계약을 추진 중이다. TerraPower 주기기 3종 중 2종 계
약이 완료되어 제작에 착수했으며, Rolls-Royce SMR·i-SMR 등 파트너십 
확대도 지속되고 있다. 이외에 대미투자 관련 기자재 발주는 미국 부지 확정 
및 한국 의회 승인 절차가 남아있어 내년 발주가 기대된다. 기존 웨스팅하우
스 AP1000 Vogtle 계약 대비 업무 범위 확대(원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 
품목) 가능성이 있어 긍정적으로 평가한다.

2_ Eugene Research Center 
설명회 Q&A 
Q1. 4Q26 SMR 수주 목표 약 1 조원의 실현 가능성 
- 고객사와 GTC 등 주요 계약 조건에 대한 합의를 대부분 완료하고 계약 체결 대기 중 
- 연내 계약 가능성이 있어 수주 목표는 의미 있는 수준

Q2. NuScale 모듈 1 기 제작 계약의 PP A 지연 영향 
- 완결된 제작 계약으로 PP A 체결이 지연되더라도 해당 모듈의 제작 및 진행에는 문제없음

Q3. AP1000 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 회계 처리 및 사업 영향 
- DOE·DOC 및 한국 정부 재원을 활용해 EPC 계약·착공 지연과 무관하게 기자재 제작 가능 
- 프로젝트 중단 시 웨스팅하우스의 다른 프로젝트로 기자재 전용 가능성 검토

Q4. 체코 원전 컨소시엄 변경 시 참여 및 사업 계획 
- 현지 지점 운영과 시공인력 상주로 시공 환경 자료 확보 
- 한수원과 초기 인허가·설계에 참여하고, 주기기 공급·설치 역량을 바탕으로 경쟁력 확보 기대

Q5. 체코 원전 EPC 사업의 수익성 확보 가능성 
- 한수원 가이드에 따라 수익성을 고려한 입찰 진행 예정

Q6. 웨스팅하우스 SPV 및 폴란드 AP1000 장주기 기자재 발주 진행 상황 
- DOE·DOC 프로젝트와 폴란드 AP1000 3기 기자재 조기 발주 협의 중 
- 연내 수주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 수주 추진

Q7. 3Q26 영업이익률 개선 요인 
- 원자력·가스터빈 중심의 사업 믹스 개선이 수익성에 기여 
- 제작 진행률과 마일스톤에 따라 이익 인식

### Page 3

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _3 
Q8. 4Q26 복합화력 수주 목표 5,000억원의 달성 가능성 
- 국내 1 건, 해외 2 건 협의 중이며 국내 프로젝트의 수주 가능성이 높은 상황 
- 대미 투자 물량은 기존 2031년까지의 예약계약 납기와 비교해 검토 중

Q9. 3Q26 에너빌리티부문 당기순손실 원인 
- 급격한 환율 하락에 따른 약 800억원의 환산손실이 주요 원인

Q10. 대미 투자 관련 기자재 발주 시점 
- 미국 부지 확정, 웨스팅하우스 사업제안 및 한국 정부 심사·국회 승인 절차 필요 
- 연말~내년 초 발주 여부는 불확실하나 조기 발주 추진

Q11. 대미 투자 프로젝트의 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 원자로·증기발생기 외 추가 품목까지 공급 범위 확대를 위해 웨스팅하우스와 협상 중

Q12. 장주기 기자재 선발주 후 인허가 지연 시 실적 영향 
- 기자재 출하 시 수금 조건이 충족되며 선발주 자금도 사전 확보 
- 보글·VC 서머 사례에서 기자재 대금을 회수해 프로젝트 지연에 따른 실적 영향은 제한적

Q13. 4Q26 신재생·풍력 수주 목표 약 7,000억원의 달성 가능성 
- 해상풍력 기자재·EPC 계약 및 유지보수 계약 추진 중 
- 주요 계약 협의가 상당 부분 완료돼 일정 지연이 없다면 연내 목표 달성 전망

Q14. 중형 가스터빈 개발 및 해외 진출 계획 
- 대형 가스터빈은 한국·미국 중심으로 추진하며 중동 고객사와도 협의 중 
- 휴스턴 서비스 허브를 기반으로 미국 데이터센터 시장 대응 
- 중형 가스터빈은 시제품 제작 및 시험 진행 후 2028년 하반기 출하 목표

### Page 4

두산에너빌리티

4_ Eugene Research Center 
Q15. NuScale 모듈 1 기 선발주 배경 
- 대규모 일괄 발주에 대비한 제작 경험 확보 및 납기 리스크 완화를 위해 사전 제작 추진

Q16. X-energy 연내 후속 계약 대상 품목 
- 증기발생기 전열관 및 주단 소재 등 장주기 품목의 사전 발주 추진

Q17. DOE 프로젝트의 장주기 기자재 공급 범위 확대 가능성 
- 웨스팅하우스 공급망 상황에 따라 추가 품목 협상 중 
- 과거 보글·VC 서머보다 넓은 공급 범위 기대

Q18. 웨스팅하우스 지분 투자와 기자재 계약 시점의 연계 가능성 
- 지분 투자는 내년, 기자재 계약은 연내 또는 내년 초 목표로 시점이 일치할지는 불확실

Q19. X-energy 장주기 기자재의 소재·부품 구분 
- 증기발생기 전열관은 부품, 주단 소재는 소재에 해당 
- 전열관은 공급 병목에 대비해 조기 발주하고 주단 소재는 설계 확정 후 제작 준비

Q20. 복합화력 주기기 수주의 기존·신규 고객 비중 
- 기존 고객과 추가 예약계약을 협의 중이며 신규 글로벌 고객과도 예약계약 협상 진행

Q21. 주요 SMR 사업 외 파트너십 확대 가능성 
- Rolls-Royce 사업 추진 및 GE Hitachi BWRX-300 주단 소재 예약계약 체결 
- i-SMR은 2035년 운전을 목표로 2028년 이전 발주가 필요해 관련 협의 진행

Q22. X-energy 전열관·주단 소재 수주 물량 규모 
- 다우케미컬 초도 사업은 80MW 원자로 4 개 모듈로 구성되며, 해당 4 개 모듈분 부품·소재 공급

### Page 5

두산에너빌리티

Eugene Research Center _5 
Q23. 중동 지정학적 불안정에 따른 프로젝트·물류 영향 
- 기존 프로젝트의 운송비 증가분을 발주처·개발사와 협의 중 
- 신규 계약에는 비용 보상 및 공기 지연 관련 조항 반영

Q24. 가스터빈 생산능력 증설 규모 및 시기 
- 현재 생산능력 증설 중이며 2031년 출하분까지 예약 접수 
- 시장 수요에 따라 추가 증설 검토

Q25. 2030년 가스터빈 예약 물량의 실제 계약 전환 시점 
- 2030년 출하 물량은 대부분 내년 실제 계약 여부 결정 전망 
- 2031년 출하 물량은 2028년경 계약 여부 결정 예상

당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 283: A股情绪见底了吗？

Source PDF: all report pdf/A股情绪见底了吗？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

中信证券认为，情绪指数于 10 ⽉ 30 ⽇跌破 10 进⼊恐慌区， A 股正式开启新⼀轮探底⾏情。
当前换⼿率仍在 1% 上⽅、融资买⼊降⾄⽜市以来最低、股债收益差创 2016 年以来最弱 ——
多项指标显⽰情绪尚未⻅底。后市或呈 “V 型底 ” 急跌抄底或 “U 型底 ” 漫⻓筑底两种演绎，救
市流动性⽀撑成关键变量。
摘要：
核⼼观点
情绪指数于 10 ⽉ 14 ⽇跌破 25 进⼊低迷区，标志着市场从震荡市转为熊市。 10 ⽉ 30 ⽇情绪指数再
次跌破 10 进⼊恐慌区，标志着 A 股将进⼊新⼀轮探底⾏情。市场陷⼊恐慌区之初可能由于被动去
杠杆或类似被动卖盘的担忧⽽出现恐慌性抛售和加速下跌的情况。要警惕市场陷⼊流动性危机
⽽出现负反馈问题，救市资⾦提供的流动性⽀持⾄关重要。当前情绪指数尚未⻅底，后续若情
绪指数⼤幅下滑并达到情绪冰点，则意味着市场到达 “V 型底 ” 底部，在最恐慌时刻将迎来最佳抄
底时机（如 24 年 1 ⽉）；若情绪指数在恐慌区窄幅震荡但未跌⾄情绪冰点，意味着市场仍将缓慢

向下探寻 “U 型底 ” 底部，需要耐⼼等待情绪指数回升⾄ 10 以上脱离恐慌区才能迎来做多机遇（如
24 年 6-9 ⽉）。
A 股情绪⻅底了吗？
换⼿率：在经历连续 3 个⽉缩量后，量能接近本轮⽜市以来最低⽔平。但当前换⼿率仍处于 1%
上⽅，意味着市场尚未充分缩量，后续存在⼆次探底⻛险。
偏股型基⾦新发量： 10 ⽉末偏股型基⾦新发量⼤幅上升⾄近年来偏⾼的⽔平，主要源⾃主动
ETF 基⾦集中发⾏的影响，剔除这⼀影响后，该指标仍处于低位。
融资买⼊占⽐：融资买⼊占⽐指标 10 ⽉初⼤幅下滑，⽉中已经降⾄本轮⽜市以来最低⽔平，下
旬窄幅震荡未能明显回升，显⽰杠杆资⾦情绪悲观。
隐含⻛险溢价：指标 10 ⽉延续上⾏趋势，从⽉初 3.08% 快速升⾄⽉末 3.51% ，显⽰权益资产性价
⽐显著提升。
股债收益差：指标 9 ⽉初快速下滑转负后基本都处于零轴下⽅， 10 ⽉末降⾄ -13.78% ，市场亏钱
效应为  2016 年  3 ⽉以来最强。
超 60MA ：指标 10 ⽉下滑后下旬已经跌破  20% 警戒线，反映市场资⾦⾯持续恶化，使得市场陷

⼊异常弱势状态（类似  201 8 年、 2024 年）。
超买超卖：8 ⽉以来⻓期处于零轴附近下⽅位置， 10 ⽉末加速下⾏跌⾄ -12.5% 附近，揭⽰当前市
场仍处于弱势震荡状态，短期可能出现技术性反弹，但⼤的超跌反弹动能仍然偏弱。
正⽂
2022 年 3 ⽉底，我们推出了中信建投策略 - 投资者情绪指数，由多个市场公开交易指标合成⽽
来。该指数在 A 股历史⾏情重要区间都较好地反映了当时市场情绪⽔平，并且极⾼和极低点能够
领先于⾏情反转，具有⼀定的预测能⼒。需要指出的是，该指数⽤于刻画市场的投资者情绪，
是同步指标，其预测性主要由投资者情绪对市场的预测性来体现。投资者情绪指数推出后，受
到了⼤量投资者的关注，因此我们从 2022 年 4 ⽉底开始，以⽉报的形式，对当前市场情绪进⾏跟
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

踪和展⽰，并给出分项核⼼情绪指标的历史⾛势和最新动向。 2024 年 8 ⽉，我们再度推出了专题
报告，回顾了情绪指数发布近两年半时间市场择时的实战效果，并总结了不同情绪状态下不同
市场⻛格的表现。
A 股情绪⻅底了吗？
10 ⽉投资者情绪指数并未根据季节效应规律在国庆节后出现回升，反⽽⼤幅下滑，这提⽰了 A 股
市场情绪异常快速下⾏和增量资⾦明显缺乏的问题。最终情绪指数于 10 ⽉ 14 ⽇跌破 25 进⼊低迷
区，结合万得全 A 指数⼤幅低于年线下⽅，标志着市场从震荡市转为熊市。 10 ⽉ 30 ⽇情绪指数再
次跌破 10 进⼊恐慌区，标志着 A 股将进⼊新⼀轮探底⾏情。
当情绪指数跌⼊恐慌区后如何应对？根据历史经验，市场陷⼊恐慌区之初可能由于被动去杠杆
或类似被动卖盘（险资、固收 + 等）的担忧⽽出现恐慌性抛售和加速下跌的情况。要警惕市场陷
⼊流动性危机⽽出现负反馈问题，救市资⾦提供的流动性⽀持⾄关重要。当前情绪指数尚未⻅
底，后续如果情绪指数⼤幅下滑并达到情绪冰点，则意味着市场到达 “V 型底 ” 的底部，在最恐慌

时刻将迎来最佳抄底时机（例如 2024 年 1 ⽉）；如果情绪指数在恐慌区窄幅震荡但未跌⾄情绪冰
点，意味着市场仍将缓慢向下探寻 “U 型底 ” 的底部，需要耐⼼等待情绪指数回升⾄ 10 以上脱离恐
慌区才能迎来做多机遇（例如 2024 年 6-9 ⽉）。
我们对各分项指标进⾏分析。七⼤指标中，换⼿率、偏股基⾦发⾏量、融资买⼊占⽐、隐含⻛
险溢价、股债收益差数据经过 5 ⽇平均平滑；超 60MA 、超买超卖两项指标原为周度数据，为了
更加灵敏现在统⼀进⾏⽇度化， 60 周均线实质上改成了 300 ⽇均线。下⽂均默认使⽤这⼀⼝径。

### Page 3

换⼿率：10 ⽉换⼿率低位震荡，但仍处于 1% 上⽅。在经历连续 3 个⽉缩量后，量能接近本轮⽜
市以来最低⽔平。但当前换⼿率仍处于 1% 上⽅，意味着市场尚未充分缩量，后续存在⼆次探底
⻛险。
偏股型基⾦新发量：剔除主动 ETF 基⾦集中发⾏影响后，偏股型基⾦新发量仍处于低位。 10 ⽉
末偏股型基⾦新发量⼤幅上升⾄近年来偏⾼的⽔平，主要源⾃主动 ETF 基⾦集中发⾏的影响，剔
除这⼀影响后，该指标仍处于低位。
融资买⼊占⽐：10 ⽉低位下滑，⽉中降⾄本轮⽜市以来最低⽔平。融资买⼊占⽐指标 10 ⽉初⼤
幅下滑，⽉中已经降⾄本轮⽜市以来最低⽔平，下旬窄幅震荡未能明显回升，显⽰杠杆资⾦情
绪悲观。

### Page 4

隐含⻛险溢价：10 ⽉⼤幅上⾏，权益资产性价⽐显著提升。指标 10 ⽉延续上⾏趋势，从⽉初
3.08% 快速升⾄⽉末 3.51% ，显⽰权益资产性价⽐显著提升。
股债收益差：10 ⽉⼤幅下⾏，⽉末降⾄ 2016 年 3 ⽉以来最低⽔平，市场亏钱效应显著。指标 9 ⽉
初快速下滑转负后基本都处于零轴下⽅， 10 ⽉末降⾄ -13.78% ，市场亏钱效应为 2016 年 3 ⽉以来
最强。
超 60MA ：10 ⽉低位下滑，下旬已经跌破 20% 警戒线，反映市场资⾦⾯持续恶化。该指标以中⻓
期视⻆刻画市场强弱状况，反映市场上收盘价在 60 周均线（ 300 ⽇均线）上⽅的个股⽐例。从历
史上看该指标超过 80%/ 低于 20% 往往意味着市场情绪的过热 / 过冷，⾏情有出现反转的可能。但
指标 10 ⽉下滑后下旬已经跌破 20% 警戒线，反映市场资⾦⾯持续恶化，使得市场陷⼊异常弱势
状态（类似 2018 年、 2024 年）。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
47 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
图⽚
超买超卖：8 ⽉以来⻓期处于零轴附近下⽅位置， 10 ⽉末加速下⾏跌⾄ -12.5% 附近。该指标以短
期视⻆刻画市场强弱情况，揭⽰当前市场仍处于弱势震荡状态，短期可能出现技术性反弹，但
⼤的超跌反弹动能仍然偏弱。
本⽂来源：中信证券

 It is

 level of

, but 
they 
have 
 deteriorat

moderate.

 , 
but

 less severe  
than expected  
so far
 . Economic 
activity

 strong
 ly

ietnam
 ’s trade balance 
 has

 in 2026. It turned in

19 
bn

 (customs data)

 in the previous year

 ing

 On the other hand, V

3

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE
: STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH AND LOW PUBLIC 
AND 
FOREIGN DEBT VS. LOW EXTERNAL 
LIQUIDITY AND 
A 
WEAK BANKING SECTOR

◼ 
 Strong factors support 
 Vietnam’s 
 overall country risk  
and economic prospects
 :  
i) 
a 
strong manufacturing export base that is moving up the value chain
 ; 
ii) 
s
olid comparative advantages that continue to attract large FDI inflows
 ; 
iii) 
c
urrent account surpluses and moderate foreign debt
 ; 
iv) 
a 
moderate government debt burden
 ; 
v) 
g
radually improving policy making
 ; 
vi) 
 s
trong medium
 -
 term economic growth prospects, assuming  
 that 
 the authorities will continue to implement structural reforms 
and strengthen productivity in the entire economy.

◼ 
 On the negative side,  
major negative factors continue to fuel some macro
 -
 financial risks:  
i) t
 he excessive domestic debt level of the economy (excluding the government) and fragilities in the banking sector 
 – 
this is a bad 
combination that fuels high credit risks and financial instability risks
 ; 
ii) 
 low forex reserves
 , which 
 have not much strengthened in recent years
 , 
 despite current account surpluses and notably due to 
resident capital flight. Forex reserves do not  
provide good  
protect
ion 
against shocks, while 
 Vietnam 
 is vulnerable to external shocks
 ;  
iii) 
 v
ery high exposure to physical climate risk.  
 C
oastal and river
 -
 delta regions, where population and economic activity are 
concentrated, 
 are 
 highly exposed to sea
 -
 level rise
 s 
and flooding
 .

◼ 
 Non transfer risk and currency risk
 : 
 V
ietnam  
 has enjoyed very 
 favourable  
 external account dynamics in recent years  
 until 2025
 , 
thanks to 
 large FDI inflows
 , 
recurrent 
 current
 -
 account surpluses  
and declining f
 oreign debt (
 which is moderate, at 
 less than  
30% of 
both GDP and exports). 
 This trend 
 has been  
interrupted 
 in 
 2026 
as 
current account surpluses 
 are 
 decreasing
 . Meanwhile
 , 
there is a 
persist
 ent 
 risk of 
 increasing 
 resident capital outflows 
 fuelling  
 some 
 VND 
 depreciation pressure  
 (
w
hereas  
 the potential for foreign 
capital outflows is limited
 )
. 
As 
the forex  
reserve cushion is weak
 , it would 
 not enable the 
 central bank 
 to 
defend the dong for a long 
time 
i
f there is  
severe currency and external liquidity pressure
 . 
However, so far this year, 
 the 
VND/USD spot rate and the reference 
rate 
 (Vietnam has a crawling band FX regime) 
 have depreciated only slightly
 , supported by strong exports and FDI inflows
 .

◼ 
 Sovereign risk
 : 
the government debt burden 
 is moderate and 
 will remain 
 so in the short term, even if the fiscal deficit is projected to 
deteriorate
 . The fiscal deficit could exceed 3% of GDP in 2026 due to support measures in response to the energy shock (estimated at 
less than 1% of GDP in H1 2026), but this will not endanger public
 -
 debt dynamics. Government debt should fall again in 2027 thanks 
to lower deficits and fast GDP growth. Debt was only 30% of GDP in 2025 (vs. 41% in 2019) and interest payments consumed less  
than 5% of fiscal revenue (vs. 7.2% in 2019). The government cover
 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is 
deepening gradually and not 
 highly 
 exposed to foreign investors. Government debt in foreign currency declined to about 25% of total 
debt in 2025 (vs. 38% in 2019); this is still substantial, but almost all this FC debt 
 is made up  
 of concessional loans from official 
creditors. On the negative front, high credit risks in the state corporate and bank sectors 
 are 
 very large contingent risks for the 
sovereign. Moreover, public investment needs related to climate change and infrastructure development will increase in the 
 medium 
term
.

◼ 
 Systemic credit risk
 : 
Domestic credit to the private sector is too large and still rising (141% of GDP in 2025 vs. 108% in 2019) while 
the banking sector remains poorly capitali
 s
ed. Credit risks and resource misallocation have long been exacerbated by opacity and 
supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022
 –
2023, banks 
faced severe funding stress and asset
 -
 quality deterioration resulting from the property and bond market crisis. The situation has 
recovered gradually since then. In the 
 short term
 , credit risks may increase amid higher energy costs and if  
there is  
a correction in 
the tech cycle. Some banks, especially small private ones, also remain exposed to VND funding risk (whereas banks do not rely  
much 
on 
 foreign
-
 currency 
 financing). Positively, reforms, including stricter capital
 -
 adequacy requirements, continue to be introduced 
gradually 
 in order 
 to improve banks’ creditworthiness (
 the 
 SBV plans to test the removal of credit quotas for the soundest banks 
from 2026).

## Document 317: daily story_FLEX_2026_10_29_C_US

### Page 1

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  硬體‧美國

Flex 
(FLEX.O/ FLEX US)

預期資料中心電源需求為獲利加速成長之主要動能 
重要訊息 
Flex FY2Q27 財報與 FY3Q27 財測均優於市場共識 ，公司上修 FY2027 資料
中心年增率自 70%至 80%，收盤股價上漲約 12%。 
評論及分析  
FY2Q27 資料中心電源需求升溫， EPS 年增 49%至 1.18 美元並優於共識。
Flex FY2Q27 (2026 年 7–9 月)營收年增 25%、季增 7%至 84.9 億美元，主
要來自資料中心電源 需求延續 ；受惠於電源產品營利率 年增 5.5ppt s 至約
16.0%、帶動產品組合轉佳， Flex FY2Q27  EPS 年增 49%、季增 18%至
1.18 美元，大致符合凱基原預估、優於市場共識 12%。 
FY3Q27 資料中心成長性可望優於 FY2Q27，預估 EPS 年增 52%至 1.32 美
元。Flex 已於 10 月完成對 EPC Power 的收購，該公司可望貢獻單季營收約
2 億美元，並有助於 800V 架構及固態變壓器( SST)的技術能力。FY 3Q27 隨
Hyperscalers 與 Neo clouds 等客戶需求延續，凱基預估 Flex 營收年增
33%、季增 11%至 93.9 億美元，其中資料中心業務之營收年增率預期達
115%，其餘產品線則約 10%上下；受惠營利率較佳的資料中心業務貢獻提
升，FY3Q27  EPS 預估年增 52%、季增 11%至 1.32 美元。 
凱基預估 Flex FY2027/FY2028  EPS 為 5.24/8.80 美元。基於電源產品接單
成長性優於預期以及併購 EPC Power 也將挹注營收，Flex 上修 FY2027 資料
中心營收年增率 自 70%至 80%，FY2028 則維持年增 80%之展望，隱含
FY2027/FY2028 資料中心營收分別上修 約 6% 、營收占比預期達
33%/45%。基於 Flex 上修展望，凱基 將 Flex FY2027/ FY2028 EPS 自原預估
之 4.96/7.96 美元上修至 5.24/8.80 美元，分別年增 59%/68%。 
投資建議  
凱基維持「增加持股」評等，目標價上修至 176 美元 (20x FY2028F EPS) 。 
投資風險  
關稅戰惡化導致客戶下單意願降低； CPI 業務營運成長態勢不如預期。

### Page 2

美國 Flex

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  2

Flex 資料中心營收占比提升至 30%。Flex 三大產品線為 ITS (包含網通與智慧
生活，FY 2Q27 營收占比 39%；營利率 5.5%)、RMS ( 包含工業、汽車與醫
療，FY1Q27 營收占比 31%；營利率 6.4%)、CPI ( 資料中心業務，FY1Q27
營收占比 30%；營利率 10.7%)。各項業務之應用主要包含:  (1) 工業產品涵
蓋資本設備、工業裝置、再生能源、電源與電網邊緣等解決方案； (2) 車用
產品主要提供下一代移動方案、自駕、連網化 、電動化與智慧科技 ；(3) 健
康解決方案包含醫療裝置、醫療設備與藥物輸送系統； (4) 智慧生活包含消
費性電子、家電、消費性包裝、掃地機器人、微型移動裝置及音訊產品； (5) 
網通領域涵蓋通訊基礎設施與企業網路傳輸； (6) 資料中心業務 包含電源、
散熱、系統整合等產品與服務。   
Flex 目前的發展策略是結合 EMS 與產品的服務策略以提升接單能力，公司
表示自身是全球唯一具有提供從資料中心晶片到電網能力的供應商，除了可
提供嵌入式電源 (embedded power) 產品、管理伺服器 GPU 的電源，亦可
滿足客戶對資料中心內所有設施的電力與散熱需求，提供完整的客製化服
務，帶動公司資料中心營收占比自前一季之 28%提升至 FY2Q27 之 30%。

圖 1: Flex CPI部門為近幾季主要成長動能，營收占比逐步提升至30%

Flex FY2027/ FY2028 資料中心營收與獲利成長性可望優於產業平均 。Flex
計劃將 CPI 部門分拆成一家新的上市公司 Axiom，維持在 CY1Q27 完成分拆
的進度不變， Flex 自身將專注於醫療保健、機器人、自動化倉儲和網通等產
業，兩家公司可更聚焦 於彼此的業務並提升資金運用效率。公司 提及自身在
資料中心電源布局相對完整，且產品價格亦相對具有競爭優勢下，使公司在
電源產品接單成長性優於產業平均； 隨包含 Google 在內的 Hyperscalers 、
Colocations 及 Neo-clouds 等客戶對嵌入式電源與 系統級解決方案的需求升
溫，公司在此次會議將 FY2027 資料中心營收年增率自 70%上修至 80%、
FY2028 則維持年增 80%之展望，皆較 FY2026 資料中心業務營收年增 38%
至 66 億美元加速成長 ，並隱含 FY2027/FY2028 資料中心營收分別上 修約
6%、營收占比預期達 33%/45%。

### Page 3

美國 Flex

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  3

獲利性部分， 受惠於電源產品營利率較佳、 FY2Q27 年增 5.5ppts 至約
16.0%，優於資料中心業務的平均營利率約 11%以及公司整體平均營利率
約 7%，公司上修 FY2027 資料中心業務之營利率約 1ppt 至 11.6%。凱基
預估隨資料中心營收貢獻提升， FY2027/FY2028 營 利占比分別達
49%/65%，為公司主要獲利動能。  
至於資料中心以外之 其餘應用，預期營收年增率 維持約 10%上下之水準。
目前歐洲工業需求略 有回溫，營收年增率可望維持約 10%上下；汽車產品
線則因目前全球汽車銷售量已逐步回升至過往 9,000 萬輛的正常水準，
FY2027 /FY2028 產品線成長性預期落在 5%以內；醫療設備則因客戶先前在
Covid -19 時期堆積的庫存水位過高，目前庫存去化雖逐步落底，但 需求端仍
相對平緩；網通領域則預期隨庫存去化力道放緩， FY2027 產品線營收將逐
步回溫，預期年增率 約 10%；消費性電子則因記憶體漲價、品牌廠將成本
轉嫁給消費者的影響， FY202 7 營收預期年減約 5-10%。 
Flex 評等維持「增加持股」，目標價上修至 176 美元。基於 Flex 上修資料
中心之營收與營 利率展望，凱基將 Flex FY2027/FY2028 EPS 自原預估之
4.96/7.96 美元上修至 5.24/8.80 美元，分別年增 59%/68%；相較市場共識
FY202 7/FY2028  EPS 預估為 4.48/6.82 美元，股價交易於約 12/14 倍之
FY2028 凱基與市場共識 EPS 預估。 
Flex 過往五年本益比多落於 5-20 倍之間，考量 (1) 公司在資料中心領域掌握
從晶片、液冷散熱、BBU 到電網的供應能力 ，布局相對多數同業更全面 ；(2) 
公司持續提升在 CSP 廠供應鏈之接單量，有助 FY2027 /FY2028 營收與獲利
加速成長； (3) 基於資料中心產品線營利率可望逐步提升至 10-15%、顯著
優於公司過往產品之 5%水準，且在獲利占比逐步提升至 50%以上的帶動
下，有助公司評價向散熱與電源同業靠攏。綜合上述 原因，評估 Flex 本益比
可望回升至區間 上緣之 20 倍上下浮動 ；基於 Flex 當前評價偏低 ，故維持
「增加持股」之投資評等，目標價隨 EPS 預估上修而調整至 176 美元 (20x 
FY202 8F EPS)。

圖 5: 公司概況  圖 6: CPI 營收占比預期自 FY2026之 24%提升至 FY2028之 45%

營收與獲利主要動能。公司 三大產品線為 ITS ( 包含網通與智慧
生活)、RMS (包含工業、汽車與醫療 )、CPI (資料中心業務，包

24%。FY2026 資料中心營收已達 66 億美元，營收比重約
24%。

### Page 7

美國 Flex

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 7

項目 定義 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用
事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下 
購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源 
再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例 
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內 
廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物 
總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮 
環保支出 環保支出總金額 - 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處置、處理、衛生、清理支出 
員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減
或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數 
女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100 
女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100 
教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作 
每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數 
股東治理分數 衡量公司用以反收購工具的有效性 
公司治理分數 衡量公司對最佳治理原則的承諾和有效性 
產品責任分數 衡量公司生產優質產品和服務的能力，且產品是否將客戶的健康、安全、整合性和數據隱私進行綜合考量 
社區關係分數 衡量公司對成為優良公民、保護公眾健康和尊重商業道德的承諾 
勞動力分數 衡量公司在員工工作滿意度、健康、工作場所的安全、多樣性、平等以及員工發展機會方面的成效 
資源使用指標 衡量公司在原物料、能源或水的使用效率，以及是否通過改進供應鏈來尋求更俱生態效率的解決方案

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• We've lifted our Q3 expectation for real PCE on incoming data and a slightly firmer consumer
backdrop in the wake of recent revisions.
Consumer spending looks firmer in the third quarter, and we now expect real PCE to rise at a 3.4%
annualized clip. This upward revision reflects recent data, with August consumption coming in firm
with real spending rising by the fastest monthly clip in nearly two years, but also reflects a slightly

firmer consumer backdrop in the wake of the BEA's annual revisions released late September. Although
revisions did not materially alter spending, they lifted the level of household income in recent years,
which also boosted the personal saving rate. The trajectory of real income growth remains weak, but
the consumer still looks to be in a slightly stronger financial position than previously believed, which
helps explain recent resilience in spending and suggests consumers may have a bit more runway ahead.

Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build out will lead to another surge in business fixed investment spending in Q3, though the
effect on headline growth will be partly offset by stronger imports.
Business fixed investment spending continues to be supported primarily by the high-tech buildout.
We forecast real equipment investment to rise at around a 16% annualized clip in Q3, but part of
this boost to headline GDP growth will be offset by stronger import growth that is facilitating the

build out. Net imports of computers are tracking to surge in the third quarter, which will provide a
sizable lift to information processing equipment investment, or around half of overall equipment.
And while non-defense capital goods shipments (including aircraft) slipped in August, the near-term
growth trajectory remains supportive of a pickup in equipment. The rebuild in inventories we have
been anticipating is also tracking to be a bit stronger as businesses replenish drawn down product.

Investment: Residential
• The abrupt rise in mortgage rates is likely to dampen residential investment over the next few
quarters. Residential spending should find support next year as longer-term interest rates
moderate somewhat.
We are unlikely to see another positive residential investment reading in Q3. Rising mortgage rates

have sent a chill over builder outlooks, pushing sales expectations to their weakest level in more than
three years. While builders have responded with deeper price cuts, affordability pressures continue
to weigh on single-family sales and construction outlays. Apartment construction is simultaneously
soft amid rising financing costs and modest rent growth, and home improvement spending remains
constrained by high materials costs and interest rates. While residential investment is likely to remain

4 | Economics

### Page 5

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
a drag this year, we expect a slight moderation in longer-term interest rates in 2027, which would
provide a more supportive backdrop.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures
• We have not made material changes to our nonresidential structures forecast. Despite elevated
interest rates, data center and related spending should continue to support structures investment
through our forecast horizon.
Nonresidential structures spending has accelerated sharply, overwhelmingly driven by data centers

and electric power facilities. As data center construction expands geographically and demand rises
for semiconductors, high-tech capex has also started to generate positive spillovers for warehousing
and manufacturing construction. This buildout, which appears relatively insensitive to interest rates,
should be enough to sustain solid growth in structures investment even as spending across most
other categories remains essentially flat. Domestic energy is another green shoot, with elevated prices

supporting ongoing growth in active rotary rigs.

Net Exports & Trade
• An expected drag from trade in Q3 got larger in light of a near-record trade deficit in August, net
exports is now expected to lop off three full percentage points from third quarter growth.

Exports increased in August for the first time in four months, but the increase was more than offset by
an even larger increase in imports. The upshot was a widening in the trade deficit to a near-record gap
of more than $105 billion. Going into the report, we had a 1.9 percentage point drag from net exports
penciled in for Q3 GDP; we revised that to a full 3.0 percentage points drag in light of the record gap.
Even a modest narrowing in the final months of the year would likely mean a lift to headline GDP in

Q4, but the real story here is that trade will remain a headwind throughout the forecast period owing
largely to surging imports of tech-related products amid the AI boom.
Labor Market
• The jobs market remains stable. We continue to expect the unemployment rate to end the year at
4.2% and hold near that level in 2027.
We modestly revised down our near-term payroll growth forecast following the September jobs
report, but otherwise we have left our labor market outlook unchanged. Measures of labor demand

growth continue to point to a stable labor market. Job openings have inched higher on a year-ago
basis but remain broadly steady while initial jobless claims, WARN notices and Challenger job cut
announcements continue to signal low layoffs. Labor force participation appears to be improving after
its sharp pullback in the first half of the year. Looking ahead, we expect payroll growth to average
around 60K per month in 2027, roughly in line with our estimate of breakeven job growth. That should

Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
keep the unemployment rate near 4.2% in 2027 and leave labor market conditions broadly consistent
with "full employment." Notably, our expectation for labor cost growth of ~3.4% is the reason we
continue to reiterate that labor cost growth is not a contributor to the excess inflation seen in the
data.
Inflation
• We expect the core PCE deflator to rise 3.2% and 2.6% Q4/Q4 in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Headline inflation remains heavily influenced by developments in the Middle East, with higher oil

prices lifting gasoline and diesel prices. Food inflation has been tame this year but looks poised to
strengthen as higher transportation and commodity prices work their way through supply chains.
Beyond food and energy, price pressures remain concentrated in a handful of categories, most notably
airfares and AI-related tech goods. Core goods inflation is showing signs of stabilizing, as waning tariff-
related price pressures have been offset by resilient demand tied to the AI buildout. We expect some

improvement here in 2027. Still-low shelter inflation and cooler price growth for airfares and health
care should help bring about some moderation in core services inflation next year. We expect the core
PCE deflator to rise 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis in 2026 before slowing to 2.6% in 2027.

Fiscal Policy
• Fiscal year (FY) 2026 ended on September 30, and the federal government tallied a $2.0 trillion
budget deficit (6.5% of GDP). We project the deficit to be unchanged in dollar terms in FY 2027

and a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).
• Another continuing resolution to extend government funding past the December 11 deadline
seems like the most likely outcome at this time for the FY 2027 budget. It is possible Republicans
use the lame duck session of Congress to increase defense spending and raise the debt ceiling.
Looking ahead to the lame duck session of Congress that will follow the midterm elections, Congress
needs to pass a bill that extends government funding past December 11 to avert a government

shutdown. Another continuing resolution that punts the issue into Q1 seems like the most likely
outcome. Perhaps more interesting will be whether Republicans attempt to use budget reconciliation
to pass one more partisan piece of legislation. If they do, we suspect the two things at the top the
agenda will be more funding for national defense and immigration enforcement priorities as well as a
debt ceiling increase. For the latter, raising it in the lame duck would remove some leverage Democrats

could have in 2027 if they recapture one or both chambers. It is still very early, but we estimate the
debt ceiling "X date" by which Congress must act or face default is sometime in late Q3/early Q4 of
next year.

### Page 7

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• Our base case forecast is for the FOMC to hike the fed funds rate by 25 bps in December and
then keep the policy rate unchanged in 2027 at 4.00%-4.25%, with risks skewed toward additional
tightening.
As we go to print, the FOMC seems more likely than not to keep the fed funds rate unchanged in
October. But given the rationale under which the Fed started this tightening cycle and the forcefulness

with which Warsh has driven home his displeasure with elevated inflation, it seems entirely plausible
to us that October will be a live meeting. At a minimum, the recent inflation data seem to make a hike
in December a lock. Past December, the outlook will be highly dependent on future inflation data. Our
expectation is that core inflation will cool gradually in 2027, and we still do not see signs of demand-
side inflation in the data. But hiking cycles have an inertial component to them, and the recent inflation

data reset the clock to zero on the Fed seeing several good inflation prints in a row. This creates upside
risk to our base case for the fed funds rate.

International Developments
• Global growth remains resilient, leaving our 2026 and 2027 forecasts unchanged. Our inflation
forecasts are also unchanged, although risks of delayed price pass-through could prolong inflation
pressures into 2027.

• Monetary policy continues to lean toward tightening across advanced economies, while emerging
market central banks are increasingly cautious, either holding rates or easing more gradually as
inflation pressures rebuild.
The balance of risks to global inflation remains tilted to the upside, complicating the monetary policy
outlook even as growth has proven more resilient than expected. Elevated energy prices, particularly
for refined petroleum products, persistent geopolitical uncertainty and El Niño-related food price

risks could prolong inflationary pressures into 2027. While second-round effects still remain relatively
contained, repeated supply shocks are testing central banks’ willingness to look through the temporary
price increases, particularly where inflation expectations remain elevated, even as higher government
bond yields are already contributing to tighter financial conditions.
Across advanced economies, central banks face increasingly difficult trade-offs as persistent inflation

pressures limit the scope for policy accommodation. The Eurozone remains particularly vulnerable
to energy supply disruptions, with elevated natural gas prices and low storage levels adding to
inflation risks. We continue to expect a 25 bps European Central Bank hike in December, followed by a
prolonged hold through 2027. Fiscal concerns are also adding to pressure, particularly in France, where
the selloff in government debt has pushed yields higher. In the U.K., resilient activity and renewed

Economics | 7

### Page 8

Monthly Economics
inflation pressures similarly underpin our expectation for a Bank of England hike in November,
followed by a hold through 2027.
Elsewhere, policy paths are becoming more differentiated. After four hikes this year brought the Cash
Rate to 4.60%, we expect the Reserve Bank of Australia to remain on hold, although renewed inflation
pressures or stronger-than-expected activity could prompt further tightening. We also still expect
the Bank of Canada to hold at 2.25%, with subdued growth weighing against energy-driven inflation

risks, although incoming inflation data over the coming weeks will be critical in determining whether
persistent price pressures warrant tightening monetary policy perhaps as early as the December
meeting. Japan remains an exception to the broader hold outlook: while the yen's recovery provides
some relief from imported inflation, elevated energy prices and import dependence continue to pose
upside risks. We expect the Bank of Japan to hike again by year-end and now expect an additional 25

bps hike in Q3-2027, bringing the terminal rate to 1.75%.
The emerging market story has increasingly shifted from easing toward patience or selective
tightening. In Asia, China's weak domestic demand and persistent property-sector strains continue to
warrant policy support, with AI-related exports providing some offset. We expect a more gradual pace
of Reserve Requirement Ratio cuts alongside targeted monetary and fiscal measures. India faces the

opposite challenge, with elevated food and energy prices and rupee depreciation strengthening the
case for further Reserve Bank of India tightening. In Latin America, peso depreciation and elevated
energy costs pose upside inflation risks for Mexico, although limited exchange-rate pass-through
should allow Banxico to remain on hold while policymakers monitor underlying inflation and inflation
expectations. In Brazil, election-related uncertainty and renewed inflation pressures are likely to delay

further easing, with cuts expected to resume in Q1-2027 once policymakers see more convincing
evidence of disinflation.

## Document 348: Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Germany _ An export-driven recovery (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

10
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AN EXPORT-DRIVEN RECOVERY
Growth, which began in the fourth quarter of 2025, gained momentum in the first half of 2026. This momentum should
continue, with expected growth of 1.1% in 2026 and 1.3% in 2027. In the short term, the main driver will still be exports,
particularly to Europe, which will contribute to restructuring industry. The impact of investment plans is expected to

strengthen in 2027, while domestic demand is likely to remain constrained by inflation (2.7% in 2026 and 2.6 % in 2027),
which will weigh on purchasing power. Fiscal stimulus and structural reforms are expected to continue to support growth.
However, the rise in the deficit and debt levels means that the increase in long-term interest rates observed so far is mostly
structural.
A RETURN TO GROWTH DRIVEN BY EXTERNAL TRADE
The first half of 2026 confirms the rebound in growth (0.4% and then

0.3% q/q in Q1 and Q2). This growth is driven by foreign trade (contribu-
tions from net exports of +0.8 pp and +0.2 pp, respectively, with +0.9 pp
on average coming from exports and -0.4 pp from imports), following
two years of contracting exports, particularly those to Europe (see be -
low). The increase in public expenditure ( see our analysis ) reflects
the implementation of investment plans, with the multiplier effect on
growth of these plans expected to increase.

In the second half of the year, growth is expected to remain robust,
pushing the annual average to 1.1% in 2026 (following 0.3% in 2025).
Exports are set to remain the main driver, thanks to continued strong
European demand, which is compensating for domestic demand that
remains sluggish. Growth of private consumption will, in fact, remain
subdued by energy inflation. Business investment is expected to be
driven by dynamic sectors (defence, technology equipment and aeros -

pace), which would limit the impact of rising interest rates.
In 2027, growth (1.3%) would be based more heavily on domestic de -
mand, with greater spillover effects from investment plans and a likely
recovery in household consumption (following a peak in inflation in
Q1 2027). At the same time, external demand is expected to remain
strong, but its contribution to growth would be lower than in 2026.
This is because the stronger growth in domestic demand would stimu-

late imports to a greater extent.
INDUSTRY IS BENEFITING FROM AI INVESTMENT AND INTRA-EUROPEAN
MOMENTUM
The business climate in industry improved significantly at the start of
the second half of the year (the manufacturing PMI has risen by 4 points
since May, reaching a four-year high in August). New export orders have
even reached their highest level since early 2022. These positive signs
put into perspective the risks associated with the summer’s climate

events (heatwaves and the historic drought on the Rhine), which dis -
rupted industrial activity and river transport.
Since early 2025, output in some sectors has rebounded, particularly in
the electronics sector (+6.4 %). Manufacturers of electrical equipment,
as well as IT and electronics firms, are benefiting in particular from
the wave of investment in data centres and defence. If this momentum
continues, it could offset some of the losses recorded in the automotive

and chemicals sectors, which have both seen the sharpest falls in out-
put since late 2017 (-12.8% and -19.4%, respectively).
The sustained increase in military spending on medical technologies
and robotics across Europe is driving a restructuring of the interme -
diate-goods sector. Companies exposed to struggling sectors are gra -
dually turning towards these new markets. However, this reorientation
is not fully offsetting the loss of historical markets.

The rebound in German industry is also being fuelled by European
demand. While exports to China (-12.7% YTD in July) and the United
States (-1.5%) have certainly continued to fall, exports to a number of
European Union countries have risen sharply, particularly in Southern
and Central Europe. This shift is benefiting machinery and transport
equipment (47.6% of goods exports, up 20.5% since January 2025) in
particular. We expect this momentum to continue in 2027, creating a

virtuous cycle that reinforces European growth.
A STAGNANT LABOUR MARKET
The situation in the German labour market has stabilised over the
past several quarters, but it is not yet showing signs of improvement.
The unemployment rate is shifting relatively little (4% in November;
+0.2 pp y/y) and remains below the Eurozone average (6.4%). Falling
employment in the manufacturing sector (-0.6% since Q4 2019) is offset
by an increase in the services sector (+0.9%). Nevertheless, the latest

business climate surveys suggest that the situation may stabilise. The
gradual retraining of the workforce in struggling sectors is helping to
meet some of the needs arising from new investment.
Generally speaking, in the longer term, pension reform should support
the labour supply. As a matter of fact, it will help to increase the num-
ber of workers gradually, notably through the phasing out of early reti-
rement at 63 and the indexing of the retirement age to life expectancy

from 2032 onwards.
GERMANY
Growth and inflation

world
Although the unemployment rate is set to remain low, wage growth
would remain limited, particularly in 2026, as negotiated wages will
offset inflation to a lesser extent than they will in 2027. The planned
increases in the minimum wage (+8% in 2026 and +5% in 2027, to
EUR 14.6 per hour), significant as they are, will have little knock-on

effect on the rest of the wage scale.
PURCHASING POWER CONTINUES TO BE SQUEEZED BY HIGH INFLATION
Wage purchasing power is expected to fall further in 2026, having
already lost 4 pp since 2022, before stabilising in 2027. The reduction
in income tax (by EUR 10 billion, or 0.2% of GDP), which will come into
force next January, is also expected to support the disposable income of
low- and middle-income households.
Inflation has been rising again since the start of the summer and is

expected to remain above 2% throughout our forecast horizon. It has
accelerated since June (+0.5 pp to +2.9% y/yin August) driven by a fur -
ther rise in energy prices (+6.7 pp to +9.4% y/y), while core inflation re-
mains stable at +2.6%. From spring 2027 onwards, the expected easing
in energy prices (particularly gas prices) should bring down inflation.
However, this decline will be limited by the inflationary effects of the
wider roll-out of investment plans, as well as by the expected rise in

food prices.
This inflation, which has been structurally higher since the onset of the
2022 energy shock, will weigh on household consumption. Furthermore,
German households’ propensity to save remains high by international
standards (with a net balance 15 points higher on average over the
last ten years, according to the European Commission), which is also
weighing on their consumption.
FISCAL STIMULUS CONTINUES
After recording a public deficit of -3% of GDP in 2025, we anticipate a

further deterioration in the public balance to -4% in 2026, then -4.2%
in 2027. The primary deficit is projected to reach -2.8% of GDP in 2026
(after -1.9% in 2025) before narrowing slightly to -2.7% in 2027. At the
same time, the interest burden is expected to rise gradually from 1.1%
of GDP in 2025 to 1.5% in 2027. This combination will result in a rapid
rise in the debt-to-GDP ratio, from 62.6% of GDP in 2025 to 66.1% in
2026 and then 69.7% in 2027, before approaching 85% by the end of the

decade.
The yield on the 10-year Bund rose above 3.5% on 14 September, its
highest level since 2007. In addition to the increase in issues linked to
extra-budgetary funds, inflationary pressures are also contributing to
this rise. A gradual easing of inflation next year would make a mode -
rate easing of sovereign yields possible in 2027. However, yields would
remain high, at 3.2% in Q4 2027, suggesting that the bulk of the rise is
structural.
At the same time, the government is continuing to implement its pro -

gramme of structural reforms, including measures to support business
investment, health-insurance reform, pension reform and an overhaul
of the tax system. A number of measures have come into force, par -
ticularly accelerated depreciation for business investment until 2027,
which will be followed by a reduction in corporation tax from 2028.
Administrative simplification is also set to lead to an overhaul of the
tax system from January 2027 1. The health-insurance reform has been

passed, while the pension reform is still under discussion, with expecta-
tions that it will be adopted by the end of the year.
1  The main measure will be a reduction of EUR 10 billion per year in income tax for individuals on low incomes, funded in part by an increase in taxes on high earners. The bill will
be submitted to Parliament in the autumn, before being implemented from 2027.

## Document 368: Lagarde’s comments point to a December ECB rate hike

Upside risks to inﬂation and downside risks to growth clearly
complicate the ECB's job. However, comments at today's press
conference suggest the ECB is set to hike again in December
Carsten Brzeski
ECB President Christine Lagarde addresses a press conference following
today's meeting
Rates on hold and Lagarde out. That’s the headline from today’s
ECB meeting. But look beyond that, and the message is more
nuanced: despite growing stagﬂationary pressures, the ECB still

looks set to hike rates again in December.
The decision to keep interest rates unchanged was not a surprise.
The main argument behind this decision is that there are currently
few knock-on eﬀects from higher headline inﬂation, while ﬁnancing
conditions, mainly bond yields, have become more restrictive.
A pause after two rate hikes this year was expected, even if macro
developments since the last meeting could have justiﬁed another
rate hike. Headline inﬂation increased again in September, and

According to Christine Lagarde, risks to the eurozone’s growth

Despite these new complications, the ECB apparently debated a

included in the September staﬀ projections.
ECB looks set to hike again in December
despite increasing stagﬂationary pressures
Looking ahead, there are three main parameters that, in our view,
will determine the ECB’s path going forward:
1. The length and intensity of the war in the Middle East. The
longer oil prices remain elevated, the higher the probability of
more rate hikes.
2. The knock-on eﬀects of higher energy prices. So far, there has

hardly been any pass-through of higher energy prices in the
rest of the economy. If this remains the case, the need for
further rate hikes drops. If it doesn’t, the possibility of rate hikes
rises.
3. France. Any solution to France's current ﬁscal woes could bring
some relief to bond markets and allow the ECB to continue
hiking rates. Without any solution, bond markets have taken

## Document 403: Quantum dictionary, part 2_ From potential to progress We go beyond the quantum basics to explore what’s next, from emerging applications to the global race for leadership. 2 min r

Source PDF: all report pdf/Quantum dictionary, part 2_ From potential to progress We go beyond the quantum basics to explore what’s next, from emerging applications to the global race for leadership. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13037420     1

Transformation 
Quantum dictionary, part 2: From potential to progress 
02 November 2026

Key takeaways 
• In the second and final part of our quantum dictionary series, we move beyond the basics to explore where quantum could go 
next. Today's quantum computers remain limited by noise and errors, but their potential grows rapidly as they scale. Emerging 
applications like quantum AI and sensing could eventually unlock new capabilities and significant economic value. 
• Quantum communication could change both how we connect quantum computers and how we protect information. A quantum 
internet could use entanglement to link quantum systems, creating additional computing power, while quantum networks could 
make eavesdropping detectable and offer new ways to securely transmit information. 
• Meanwhile, the race for quantum leadership is already underway. Governments are pursuing greater quantum sovereignty as the 
technology's potential implications for competitiveness, security and defense grow.

Assessing quantum’s performance and progress 
Quantum advantage versus supremacy 
Quantum advantage is reached when a quantum computer can solve a useful, real-world problem faster than a classical 
computer. The problem is, today’s quantum computers are still too noisy, as we discussed in part one of this series, and qubit 
quality isn’t yet high enough to demonstrate quantum advantage.  
Quantum supremacy takes this one step further. It refers to the point when a quantum computer has computational advantage, 
meaning it can solve a problem that a traditional computer couldn’t in any feasible amount of time.  
Quantum scaling 
Quantum scaling is about building bigger, more powerful quantum computers. It involves overcoming the challenges of 
increasing both the number and the quality of qubits. Today, we’re in the noisy intermediate-scale quantum era. Quantum 
computers have tens to a few hundred qubits, but noise and errors still limit what they can do.1 
However, when quantum computers do scale, they do so exponentially. Every additional qubit doubles the number of operations 
the system can perform. A quantum computer with just 10 qubits could perform more than 100 times the operations of a 
classical computer (Exhibit 1). More qubits could therefore mean more operations and, ultimately, more economic value in the 
industries where quantum computing can be put to work, according to BofA Global Research. 
Exhibit 1: A quantum computer with just 10 qubits could perform more than 100 times the operations than classical 
Number of classical versus quantum operations for a given (qu)bit number (log scale)

  I NSTI TUTE

Quantum Benchmarking Initiative 
The Defense Advanced Research Projects Agency’s (DARPA’s) Quantum Benchmarking Initiative (QBI) aims to answer a big 
question: Is it possible to build a commercially relevant quantum computer by 2033? To be clear, QBI is not a competition 
between companies. DARPA evaluates all viable approaches for which funding is available.  
QBI has three stages:  
• Stage A: Start with a concept. At this stage, there is a utility-scale quantum computer concept that has a plausible path to 
realization in the near term. 
• Stage B: Put a plan behind it. This stage involves developing a research and development plan, identifying the associated 
risks and introducing prototypes to overcome them. 
• Stage C: Put it to the test. In the final stage, companies work with the government to verify and validate that the utility-
scale quantum computer concept can be built as intended. 
Exploring emerging quantum applications  
Quantum AI 
According to BofA Global Research, quantum AI brings together two powerful technologies: AI and quantum computing, with the 
potential to improve both the physical and the digital worlds. And the relationship could work both ways. Quantum computing 
could provide some of the compute power needed to train generative AI, while generative AI could help speed up the 
development and testing time of quantum systems.  
AI tools like machine learning (ML) could also make quantum error correction (QEC) more efficient and scalable.2 ML models 
trained on simulated datasets can identify and correct errors faster than traditional methods while using fewer computing 
resources. They can also more accurately predict errors before they happen.3 
Quantum sensing 
Quantum sensing uses the properties of quantum mechanics to measure things like magnetic fields, gravitational forces, time 
and rotation with greater precision than classical instruments (Exhibit 2).4 
Exhibit 2: Quantum sensors use quantum properties to measure physical quantities with unmatched precision 
An infographic of quantum sensing

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

2 Wang, Z. & Tang, H. (2024, December 29). Artificial Intelligence for Quantum Error Correction: A Comprehensive Review. arXiv. 
3 Swayne, M. (2025, January 6). AI for Quantum Error Correction: A Comprehensive Guide to Using Artificial Intelligence to Improve Quantum Error Correction. 
Quantum Insider.  
4 Rehman, M.U. (2026, March 2). Understanding Quantum Sensing and Its Industrial Potential. Quantum Insider.

### Page 3

02 November 2026    3

I NSTI TUTE

So, how does it work? In simple terms, there are three steps. First, a quantum system is prepared in a well-defined initial state, 
for example, by cooling an atom to near absolute zero. Next, the sensor is exposed to whatever is being measured, causing a 
subtle shift in its quantum state. In an atomic magnetometer, for example, a magnetic field changes the energy levels of an 
atom’s electrons, which changes how the atom absorbs or emits light. Finally, the sensor measures how much the quantum state 
has shifted, revealing the strength of the external force.5  
Benefits and challenges 
Compared with quantum computers, quantum sensors face some lower technical barriers. They don’t need nearly as many qubits 
to be useful, entanglement can help but isn’t required for every application, and even short coherence times can produce useful 
measurements.6 The catch? They often require large vacuum chambers, laser systems and magnetic shielding to maintain 
coherence. 
The quantum internet and communications  
Quantum communication 
Quantum communication is the study of how information can be encoded and transmitted between quantum systems over a 
distance (Exhibit 3). It uses properties like superposition and entanglement to enable the secure transmission of information. 
Why does that matter? One of the main goals is to keep information secure from potential eavesdroppers, particularly as more 
powerful quantum computers could pose new challenges for traditional communication methods. The ultimate goal is what some 
call the quantum internet, where quantum signals can move seamlessly across many quantum networks.7 
Exhibit 3: Quantum communication uses quantum states to encode and transmit information 
An infographic of quantum communication

Source: BofA Global Research  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Quantum internet 
Think of the quantum internet as a scalable network that allows two or more quantum devices to exchange information (Exhibit 
4). These connections could span vastly different distances, from within a silicon chip all the way to satellite links.8 
But the quantum internet isn’t meant to replace the internet we use today, nor is it simply a faster version of it. According to 
Cisco, the classical internet can move information between computers, but it is ultimately limited by what those computers can 
compute. Some problems become so complex so quickly that no classical machine can solve them in a useful amount of time.

5 Ibid.  
6 Ibid.  
7 Quantinuum. (2024, July 30). Coming Over the Horizon: Quantum Communication Enters the Mainstream. 
8 Imperial. (n.d.). Quantum Internet.

### Page 4

4 02 November 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

When connection becomes computation 
Here’s what makes the quantum internet different: the connection itself can create new computing power. Using entanglement, 
a quantum network can connect quantum systems into a single, larger computing system that scales exponentially.9 That’s 
fundamentally different from classical computers, which communicate by passing copies of information back and forth. Quantum 
computers connected through a quantum network can instead create a feedback loop of entanglement and a shared space of 
possibilities that neither could produce on its own. 
Different hardware from classical 
Building a quantum internet requires a different set of hardware than the routers and switches that power today’s internet. Key 
pieces include:10   
• Quantum communication channels: These carry quantum signals, typically through fiber-optic cables or satellite links 
using free-space optics. 
• Quantum memory: To keep the network in sync, the system must catch a photon and store its quantum state in a 
stationary atom without destroying it. 
• Quantum repeaters: These help quantum signals travel longer distances by amplifying signals and reducing error rates. 
According to QuEra, the lack of viable quantum repeaters is currently a key limitation to building a global quantum 
network. 
Exhibit 4: A network of quantum computers which could send and receive information encoded in quantum states 
An infographic explaining the quantum internet

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Quantum networking 
Quantum networking is a way of sending information using quantum states. It generally includes three network types: quantum 
key distribution (QKD) for secure communications (more on this below), the quantum internet for distributed quantum 
computing, and quantum sensing networks that use entanglement to improve measurements.  
Classical networks turn information into electrical or optical signals that can be copied or intercepted without the sender or 
receiver knowing. Quantum networks work differently. Because information is transmitted using quantum states, it cannot be

9 Chan, M. (2026, June 1). Quantum internet 101: What it is, and why it matters. Cisco. 
10 QuEra. (n.d.) Quantum Internet.

### Page 5

02 November 2026    5

I NSTI TUTE

intercepted and copied without disturbing the original information. That disturbance makes an eavesdropping attempt 
detectable. 
Evaluating security & strategic risks 
Quantum encryption 
Quantum encryption uses the principles of quantum mechanics, including superposition and entanglement, to encrypt and 
securely transmit data (Exhibit 5). 
One potential risk of quantum computing is that it could eventually break widely used forms of encryption. And while large-scale 
quantum computers are still being developed, organizations may need to start preparing for that possibility today.  
Exhibit 5: Examples include quantum key distribution and post-quantum cryptography 
An infographic illustrating quantum encryption

Source: BofA Global Research 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Quantum key distribution 
Quantum key distribution (QKD), a type of quantum encryption, uses the laws of quantum physics to create and distribute secure 
keys which prevent data decryption.11 Think of a key as something that locks and unlocks a digital message. Two parties share a 
key to encrypt and decrypt information sent between them. If an eavesdropper tries to obtain the key, the attempt disturbs the 
quantum states, alerting the parties that the key may have been compromised and shouldn’t be used.12 
According to Quantum Insider, QKD generally involves three steps:13 
• Create entangled photons: First, a quantum source, such as a laser and nonlinear crystal or a quantum dot, generates pairs 
of entangled photons. The photons are correlated in ways that classical light cannot replicate, so measurements of one 
reveal information about its partner.  
• Send the photons: The photons travel to different locations through fiber-optic cables or free-space optical channels, 
such as satellite-to-ground or satellite-to-satellite links, while maintaining their quantum correlations. But those 
connections can be lost over long distances, with current systems reaching roughly 100 kilometers, according to Quantum 
Insider.14 Classical networks get around distance limitations with repeaters that receive, amplify and retransmit signals.

11 Toshiba. (n.d.). Quantum Key Distribution (QKD). 
12 QuEra. (n.d.). Quantum Key Distribution. 
13 Rehman, M.U. (2026, March 9). Understanding Quantum Networking and Its Industrial Potential. Quantum Insider. 
14 Ibid.

### Page 6

6 02 November 2026 
  I NSTI TUTE

Quantum signals can’t simply be amplified without destroying their quantum properties, so quantum repeaters take a 
different approach. They use entanglement swapping and quantum memory to extend the range of quantum networks. 
• Measure the photons: Finally, the photons are measured at each location. This confirms whether the entanglement 
survived the journey and can reveal whether someone tried to intercept the photons along the way. The results can then 
be used to generate a shared secret key for encrypting classical messages. 
Quantum sovereignty 
Quantum sovereignty is an economic actor’s ability to develop, produce and operate its own quantum technology.  
Quantum is the first new computing platform technology since the arrival of digital computing. And the stakes are high. 
Stakeholders are interested in quantum because of its potential implications for economic competitiveness, security, supply 
chain resilience and defense. As a result, some may pursue “sovereign quantum,” meaning quantum capabilities are owned and 
operated by a particular nation. That could involve investing in quantum technology, building domestic expertise or even export 
controls to limit other stakeholders’ ability to develop their own sovereign capabilities.  
The geopolitical race is on 
Government quantum investment has reached at least about $58 billion cumulatively as of 2025, led by Europe at $15.4 billion 
and China at $15.3 billion.  
The competition extends beyond investment. At least nine countries implemented export controls on quantum technology in 
2024. Over the past 20 years, China and the US have led in patent requests filed, but Europe and China have led in the number 
of patents granted. The race for talent is global, too. China has trained the largest share of top quantum researchers at the 
undergraduate level, at roughly one-fourth, while the US is home to the largest share of top talent by employment, at one-third.  
Quantum supply chain 
Building quantum technology requires much more than qubits. A broader supply chain provides the equipment, components and 
materials needed to make quantum systems work, including: 
• Cryogenics: Most types of qubits require some level of extreme cooling, with superconducting qubits among the most 
demanding at roughly 5–10 millikelvin. Keeping qubits this cold helps shield them from environmental noise that can 
cause decoherence. 
• Qubit control electronics: Quantum gates direct qubits by sending them photons. That requires supporting electronics, 
including wiring, room-temperature electronics and amplifiers. For example, analog microwave signals used to read qubits 
must be amplified through several stages before they can be converted into digital signals and analyzed. 
• Photonics: Lasers use light and atoms to help power quantum technology, while other devices can modify properties of 
photons, such as their path and polarization. 
• Foundries: Quantum development also relies on several types of fabrication facilities, including research fabs at national 
research organizations and universities, pre-industry research fabs, independent foundries that manufacture 
semiconductors for third parties, and in-house fabs operated by quantum technology vendors. 
• Raw materials: Quantum systems also depend on materials used in qubits themselves and in supporting components such 
as cables and cryostats. Helium-3 and helium-4 are used in cryostats to maintain very low temperatures, while rare earth 
metals used in quantum technology include yttrium, neodymium, europium, erbium and ytterbium.15 
AI singularity 
AI singularity refers to a hypothetical point when AI evolves to become self-improving and autonomous, surpassing human 
cognitive abilities.16  
A complementary relationship between quantum and AI 
Rather than developing in isolation, AI and quantum computing could ultimately help advance each other. Quantum computing 
may eventually provide some of the compute power needed to train generative AI, while generative AI could help speed up the 
development and testing of quantum systems.

15 Swayne, M. (2025, October 15). Rare-Earth Minerals And Quantum Computing: How Secure Are The Supply Chains? Quantum Insider. 
16 ISB. (2025, March). What is AI Singularity? Explore Its Future Impact.

### Page 7

02 November 2026    7

I NSTI TUTE

That relationship could become increasingly important as the computing power needed to train the largest generative AI models 
continues to grow exponentially. Classical computing may eventually struggle to keep pace, while quantum computing could help 
meet some of that demand because its computing power also scales exponentially. 
By 2045? 2029? Now? 
AI is becoming increasingly powerful and capable, raising a big question: when could it reach or even surpass human intelligence? 
Futurologist Ray Kurzweil expects AI to reach human levels of intelligence by 2029 and technological singularity by 2045. His 
view extends Moore’s Law beyond processing power to other forms of technological progress. 
Under that framework, computing power would continue growing exponentially. By 2045, Kurzweil projects that $1,000 worth of 
computing could perform as many calculations in one second as all human brains combined.

## Document 466: What the US midterm elections mean for healthcare and pharma

Source PDF: all report pdf/What the US midterm elections mean for healthcare and pharma.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-28

### Page 1

Rising fuel prices and the war with Iran have signiﬁcantly
increased the chances of a Democrat-controlled Congress. This
could mean greater pricing pressure on pharma and more
healthcare aﬀordability measures. However, surprises do
happen. We therefore assess three possible scenarios and what
they mean for healthcare and pharma
Diederik Stadig
Republicans and Democrats want more domestic manufacturing and lower
drug prices, but there are diﬀerences in how they get there

### Page 2

The US midterm elections take place on 3 November. All 435 seats
in the House of Representatives and 35 of the 100 Senate seats are
up for election. With Trump’s approval rating hitting a record low
according to a Fox News poll, it is no wonder that the pollsters
currently suggest the Democrats lead by around 8 percentage
points nationally. A Democratic takeover of Congress therefore
seems most likely, but a Democratic clean sweep is not certain.

We look at what the possible outcomes could mean for healthcare
and pharma.
The key differences
For healthcare, US voters face a clear choice: Democrats want to
expand Medicaid and insurance coverage, as well as permanently
extend Aﬀordable Care Act (ACA) subsidies. Republicans favour the
opposite approach, with greater responsibility shifted to individual
states.
For pharma, on the other hand, the old political divide of
"Democrats regulate, Republicans leave industry alone" is largely

gone. Both parties now see the pharma and biotech sector as key
to national security, and therefore want more domestic
manufacturing. They also both want lower drug prices. The
diﬀerence is how they get there. Democrats lean toward
government negotiation under Medicare and incentivising domestic
manufacturing through other means than tariﬀs; Republicans lean
toward most-favoured-nation (MFN) pricing and trade pressure. In
short, the policy risks diﬀer slightly depending on the outcome, but

the objectives are largely bipartisan.

on US consumers and Europe as well as
healthcare and pharma
